User: It is now 11/1/2026. Can you please read through the non-summary `*.record.md` for all October 2026 chats to bootstrap this one? I know a summary was already generated for the last. Today I want you, Astra, to take a very opinionated look at the latest work that has come from Fable in the past few days. Rebuild, look it all over, and tell me what you see. I've seen a lot of screenshots, but the one-liner left for me included only a sample of history. I want to see you explore this software as if you were an end user and tell me where it sucks for you as an end user. We've done one such exercise like this in October, and it was phenomenally successful at improving our software. While Fable is phenomenal with non-vision code/architecture tasks, Astra (you) excel far more when vision and taste are required. You are currently the LLM model with the most human-like vision capabilities as best I can tell, and that has made your opinions on the explorer immensely valuable in the past. 

Codex: I’ll read the October records in full, rebuild the Explorer with all available history, and use it through real user journeys. I’ll give you a candid critique grounded in what I can see and do, with screenshots of the most consequential problems.

The external T3 trial is an important warning in these records: a map can contain a great deal of correct data and still offer no useful starting point. I’ll judge the new discovery behavior by whether it helps me find and follow something meaningful, not simply whether it draws fewer objects.

I’ve reached the last two October records. The key recent change is now clear: discovery changes which neighborhood I see, while the renderer repairs change how quickly I can use it. I’ll test those separately—especially whether inspecting a file unexpectedly changes the scene I was trying to understand.

I’ve read all 27 October records in full. The rebuilt Explorer includes all 694 commits.

I’m moving into the browser now: first arrival, discovering and retaining a neighborhood, switching perspectives, then watching and scrubbing the full history. I’ll also compare the small estate so graph size doesn’t excuse awkward controls.

Ran terminal command: node /tmp/live-docs-nov1-review.cjs



The first hands-on problem is a conflict between discovery and reading: inspecting a neighbor expands the graph, but the reader covers much of that expansion; closing the reader removes it again. Search is noticeably better, and the history speed control is easier to reach. I’m checking these at a larger viewport before judging how much is caused by the narrow embedded browser.

const fs=require('fs'),path=require('path');
const files=['AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-01/README.md','AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/README.md','AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md'];
let links=0;const failures=[];
for(const file of files){const body=fs.readFileSync(file,'utf8').replace(/"'^(`{3,}|~{3,})[''^'"\n]*\n[\s\S]*?"'^'"\1\s*"'$/gm,'"'');for(const m of body.matchAll(/"'!?'"\[["'^'"\]]*\]\((["'^)]+)'"\)/g)){const href=m[1];if(/"'^[a-z]+:/i.test(href))continue;const [name,anchor]=href.split('"'#');const target=path.resolve(path.dirname(file),name);links++;if("'!fs.existsSync(target)){failures.push({file,href});continue;}if(anchor){const slug=s=>s.toLowerCase().replace(/[''^'"\p{L}\p{N}\s_-]/gu,'').replace(/ /g,'-');const heads=[...fs.readFileSync(target,'utf8').matchAll(/"'^#{1,6} +(.+)$/gm)].map(m=>slug(m[1]));if(!heads.includes(anchor))failures.push({file,href,reason:'"'anchor'});}}}
const dir=path.dirname(files[0]),report=fs.readFileSync(files[0],'utf8'),pics=fs.readdirSync(dir).filter(n=>n.endsWith('.png'));for(const n of pics)if("'!report.includes('"']('+n+')'))failures.push({file:n,reason:'not inventoried'});
console.log(JSON.stringify({files:files.length,localLinks:links,pictures:pics.length,failures},null,2));if(failures.length)process.exitCode=1;

Completed with input: update: /workspaces/Live-Documentation/AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md



The full timeline exposes a larger problem than the short sample: playback can grow the graph far beyond the camera’s frame, leaving me inside a moving mass of lines. Hiding commit details helps, but then I mostly see motion rather than understand a change. I’m testing Fit, slower playback, and the different emphasis modes to separate recoverable framing from missing explanation.


I found a concrete history inconsistency: immediately before the January 12 removal of GraphStore, searching for `graphStore` returns “Nothing found,” even though those files exist in the displayed historical graph. The removal itself is dramatic and useful to see, but investigating what disappeared is much harder than watching it disappear.


At desktop size, the discovery problem remains: I can open a neighbor’s documentation and lose sight of the very area that expanded. The pin controls preserve it successfully, but that extra step is doing a lot of work.

I’ve also rebuilt T3 with the current client to check whether the new starting neighborhood makes that large repository approachable.


The T3 comparison makes my judgment clearer. Its default start is still a white knot of 9,341 files. Searching for a known service gives 441; reducing the public “Hops Shown” control to one gives 33, with names and connections I can actually follow.

So discovery is a useful improvement. The remaining problem is choosing a useful beginning and amount of context without requiring the user to already know the answer—or tune the map first.


The review is now written with screenshots and reproducible journeys. My strongest recommendation is to fix historical discovery and ordinary arrival framing, then revisit how inspection retains newly discovered context. The new scope controls are useful; the next gains should come from making those everyday journeys work without recovery steps.

Ran terminal command: git status --short



I read **all 27 October records in full**, rebuilt the repository Explorer, estate and T3, and played the **complete 694-commit history**. I also scrubbed individual changes and used search, pins, inspection, both perspectives, orbit, zoom and recovery controls.

The `full review contains 25 screenshots and reproduction steps`, plus a full-history rebuild/hosting command.

**My judgment: discovery is a substantial improvement. The Explorer still makes me work too hard to keep my place and turn its pictures into answers.** These are the problems I would prioritize.

1. **History search can contradict the historical picture.**  
   Immediately before the January 12 GraphStore removal, searching `graphStore` returns **“Nothing found.”** Advance one commit and the panel lists its deletion among 58 removed files. Those deleted entries are also noninteractive. I can watch an old subsystem disappear, but investigating that subsystem is frustratingly difficult. `Evidence`.

2. **Reading something undoes the exploration I wanted to perform.**  
   A generator neighborhood contains 124 files. Inspecting a neighbor expands it to 168—but the reader covers the newly relevant area. Closing the reader shrinks it back to 124. Pinning before closing preserves it, correctly, but that extra operation carries too much responsibility. **Dismissing a document should not feel like losing my exploration.** This remains awkward at `1600×1000`, not just in the narrow embedded browser.

3. **Basic 2D arrival framing still fails.**  
   At an ordinary 1280×800, opening Portal puts most of it beyond the right edge and leaves a large blank canvas. Reset Zoom immediately fits the same content at reading size. Compare `opening it` with `pressing Reset`. That recovery should be the ordinary arrival. The repository root also wastes considerable space between staggered columns.

4. **Two hops does not guarantee a useful picture.**  
   T3’s default start is still `a white knot of 9,341 files`. Searching for a service whose name I already knew gives 441. Reducing Hops Shown to one gives `33 readable, useful subjects`. That progression demonstrates both the value of discovery and the weakness of its starting policy. A newcomer should not need prior source knowledge and tuning to find the useful experience.

5. **The full timeline needs better navigation and framing.**  
   During playback, the growing graph extended beyond the camera until I was `inside a mass of connections`. Fit recovered it. Meanwhile, the date axis spends substantial width on the development gap and compresses recent work. I want to find an event, examine it, and move around it deliberately; that remains cumbersome.

6. **The change emphasis does not yet explain the change well enough.**  
   The GraphStore star breaking apart is excellent visual evidence—58 files and 824 wires disappear. But pale surviving dots do not tell me what vanished. In the latest discovery commit, the panel names six created files while the graph prominently labels unrelated fixtures.  
   
   **My preference among the current treatments is “Dim the rest.”** It preserves familiar file colours. Glow washes them out. More important than that preference: changed identities need readable names, and removals need an understandable before/after relationship.

7. **Structure’s large folders still obscure their contents.**  
   The `repository start` is dominated by orange ancestor spheres. Their recursively counted contents are mostly hidden, yet their size obscures the few members being shown. The smaller estate works considerably better. I would finish the discussed uniform-folder treatment and judge it through navigation.

There are clear successes: filename ranking is better; reverse-path recovery works visibly in 3D; the history panel can finally get out of the way; speed is conveniently beside Play; counts agree; and small 3D neighborhoods are useful. **Those improvements survived actual use.**

I would tackle historical discoverability and ordinary framing first, then reconcile reading with retained discovery. After that, improve changed-file identity and the starting neighborhood. Those journeys matter more now than another general tuning pass.

The review is committed as `72601209`. Documentation checks passed, the workspace is clean, and **no product code changed**.

User: These critiques are absolutely phenomoenal. I saw your screenshots and agree wholeheartedly with the critiques you've leveled. Like I often say, what we are trying to build is something that maximally benefits from the common sense of LLMs. We are trying to build something very very common-sense: we want to be able to look at directories and understand their contents well, especially if those contents have interconnectivity of some kind. 

The only other thing that I feel like we've punted on repeatedly is ensuring that in the structure mode of the 3D local map, we still have differentially-sized directory nodes (the sizing pass appears to have still not yet been completed). 

Go ahead and tackle the pain points as you see them. We are really well aligned. 

Codex: I’ll take this on as a coordinated usability pass, starting with uniform directory nodes and reliable arrival framing. Then I’ll address historical search, keeping discovered context while reading, and making history’s changes easier to identify and navigate. I’ll update the documented behavior, inspect each change in the browser, and run the full verification suite.

The directory-sizing pass is still unfinished; I’ll make that an explicit part of this work.

For a repository with no declared entry point, I recommend starting at the directory root in Structure, so the person chooses an area before entering Connections. The alternative is keeping the most-connected-file start but limiting its displayed neighborhood, with an explicit way to reveal more. Which should we pursue? This changes the earlier fallback decision, so I’m bringing this design fork to you under AGENTS.md while continuing the other repairs.
- Directory-first arrival (Recommended)
- Bounded file neighborhood

The rebuilt Structure start is visibly clearer: the root and folders now read as junctions, and their branches are no longer hidden behind large ancestor spheres. The sizing checks pass.

For history, the engine already knows how to follow a file across arbitrary commits, including deletion and reuse of the same path. I’ll reuse that identity tracking so a past-only file can be searched, retained and shared.

Historical search and the timeline changes now build, and the focused identity, navigation, camera and address tests pass. I’m checking the interactions together next: finding a deletion, stepping back into the removed file, narrowing the timeline, then closing the reader without losing the neighborhood.

The first combined browser run caught a real responsive-layout fault: my timeline resize handler triggered the page’s error state when the reader narrowed the canvas. I’ve replaced that handler with CSS container sizing. The next build will verify the narrow layout without an observer changing its own dimensions.

['A return from 3D holds the subject\'s name where its sphere stood through the motion, then slides the picture the least that brings it into the frame.','A return from 3D holds the subject\'s name where its sphere stood through the motion, then slides the picture the least that brings it into the frame. ['glowing, their colour lifted toward white (the default), the rest dimmed, both, or neither','the rest dimmed (the default since November 1), glowing with colour lifted toward white, both, or neither'],
['at a moment before every pin\'s file existed the picture is that moment\'s start; a sphere whose file is gone today can be inspected, and the picture grows from it while the moment is shown, but not pinned.','when all explicitly requested files are absent, the picture stays empty and the retained controls explain their absence. ['Hubs are amber, and a hub\'s volume is the sum of its members\' volumes, so that size means exposed surface at every scale.','Hubs are amber and have uniform physical volume (four baseline units), independent of depth, descendants and file symbol scaling. ['Back between evidence targets leaves the map DOM and camera intact.','Back between evidence targets preserves the subject and pins; a change of discovered context can update the 3D scope and its framing. Evidence-only navigation leaves the map DOM intact.']
];for(const [a,b] of replacements){if("'!t.includes(a))throw Error('"'missing '+a);t=t.replace(a,b);}const marker='**Connections or Structure**';const extra='Since the [November 1 repair](../../AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-2), the date/hash readout opens commit search by hash, displayed date or message. Direct seeking outside the range restores the complete range; playback and keyboard ends respect the selected interval. Range selection is temporary presentation state, while the selected commit still travels in the address. Search and explicit investigation pause playback. Changed file names take precedence over incidental names; Structure also marks the containing folders of changes and removals. edit('persistence/compressed-url-state.ts',t=>t.replace('Missing files, directories and pins are scrubbed against the current graph, and so is a destination whose file is gone or whose directory holds no live file.','Missing files, directories and pins are scrubbed against the selection lookup: today’s graph plus validated historical identities when the bundle carries history. The settling timeout has a concrete cause: Clear replaced all 11 files with a new 191-file neighborhood, but the renderer tethered that new graph to its starting seed as though it were preserving an existing layout. In the headless reproduction that took 1,504 ticks; letting the new graph unfold took 322. I’m applying that distinction while preserving known positions when they exist.

User: I'm over here playing around with the live build you've got up. I'm noticing that the history viewer is brightening the nodes _ramping in_ rather than _ramping out_. What I mean by that is this: As a commit is proceeded through in the history viewer, (i.e. at 1 step/s or 1/2 step per second) the salient nodes to a given commit are brightening until the next commit checkpoint render arrives when all dim again. That's at least the visual impression I get. 

What I would have expected is that any node which was changed in a commit simply glows white for a turn. And then the next commit to play has them -- y'know -- back to their regular color. Wires could un-dim if something happened on them, that's fine (and I certainly expect that when a wire's strength increases or decreases with more or less coupling between nodes, the length of that connector would change in 3D space accordingly). If the version in the VS Code workspace being shared now is the latest, that's a major flaw worth resolving. 

Codex: That build includes the current repairs. The temporal emphasis still fades within each step, which can make dimmed nodes appear to brighten as the next commit approaches.

I’ll change it to your stated behavior: changed nodes glow white immediately and hold that emphasis for the whole commit; the next commit restores their normal colour unless they changed again. I’ll check the result at both playback speeds you mentioned.

Ohhh I see. No, imagine history as a network of occasionally firing fireflies. Try to maximize surprise. THe sudden dim-and-undim is awful in practice. We can still play with dimming, but just not in this order.

Codex: Understood: a steady network, with sharp, local flashes when something changes.

Changed nodes will light up white immediately for that commit, then return to their base colour. Optional dimming will provide a steady background between events. The whole scene won’t gradually brighten and reset.

The next commit replaces the changed set; playback time does not alter brightness.\n\n### Notes\nCreated for the [October 31 change-emphasis request](../../../../../../../AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/10/2026-10-31.1.record.md#turn-8), then corrected after the owner's [November 1 live playback observation](../../../../../../../AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-3): the old fade reduced darkening over an interval, making unaffected nodes steadily brighten before the next commit reset them. The owner's metaphor is a network of occasionally firing fireflies, with sharp local events against a steady field.\n\nGlow uses white for the whole displayed commit. Dim leaves unaffected nodes and wires at a fifth of their base alpha; Both combines the two; None keeps ordinary appearance. A changed wire can rise to .85 alpha. There is no clock or play-state input. Pair keys are undirected so both endpoints name the same counted wire.\n\n"'`);
authored(base+'"'views/forceGraphEmphasis.test.ts.mdmd.md',t=>t.slice(0,t.indexOf('## Authored'))+"'`## Authored'"\n### Purpose\nVerify that commit emphasis uses immediate white, restores normal colour when a node leaves the changed set, and preserves the chosen background alpha without a temporal ramp.\n\n### Notes\nThe [November 1 live correction](../../../../../../../AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-3) replaced the original linear-fade assertions. The fix removes the animation clock from change emphasis altogether. That makes the behavior independent of playback speed: a commit supplies a set of changed nodes, and that set holds until the next commit. I’m also preserving the timing measurements with the screenshots so the handoff records what actually happened between frames.


The regenerated docs and lint pass. The broader repairs now have checks for the journeys that were failing: opening Portal without a recovery zoom, keeping a discovered neighborhood after closing its reader, and reopening deleted files through shared history links. The full build and test run is continuing.


All 1,529 unit tests pass. The checks also confirm that uniform folder sizing preserves file-size differences, and that historical identities survive renames without being confused with a later file at the same path. Integration and browser verification are next.


The 113 integration tests and all documentation audits pass too. The suite is now rebuilding the browser test sites and running the complete interaction checks.

The full history remains included. Selecting a timeline range only gives that interval more room; it does not discard commits.


The browser run has passed the navigation and shared-link journeys so far, including returning to an opened folder. I’m letting the full suite finish before committing. The separate playback checks already verify the new emphasis at several points within each commit, including the final commit and closing history.


The browser confirms the directory repair in the rendered geometry: root, parent and child folders have the same physical size, while files still vary by symbol count. Opening, releasing and reframing directories also pass. I’m now through to the discovery and camera journeys.


The full browser run has now passed the longer discovery journey: closing the reader keeps the neighborhood, and a fresh shared link restores it. The remaining unfinished design choice is the no-entry starting view for repositories such as T3; that default is still unchanged.


The camera checks pass, including keeping a manual pan through a 2D/3D round trip. The earlier Clear problem also passes under its original time limit: a completely new neighborhood now unfolds normally instead of being tethered to arbitrary starting positions.


The full run caught a failure in the initial 3D reveal test. I’m inspecting the failure before deciding whether it is a rendering regression or an incorrect measurement. The suite is continuing through the other cases; I won’t count this run as a pass.


That failure was in the test’s startup assumption. The trace shows “idle” before any frame was drawn, then one uninterrupted busy period through frame 527. The reveal itself passed every size and membership check.

I corrected the test to allow only that initial, undrawn idle state. Any interruption after drawing begins still fails.


Tracing that startup further exposed a better fix: after adding the first nodes, the renderer marked its simulation busy but left the page’s status saying idle until the next frame. I’ve synchronized those states immediately and restored the original test assertion. The reveal rule and test limits stay intact.


All seven history-emphasis checks have now passed inside the full run, including the timed samples at 1 and 0.5 step/s. Historical absence messages and pathfinding through the displayed moment pass as well. So far, the startup status lag is the only additional finding.


The full run now passes the historical-file journeys too: search and inspect a deleted file, restore it in a fresh shared link, distinguish a later file at the same path, and return to the present with its absence explained. An unborn selection also stays empty and idle until its file appears.


The reader, keyboard-access and layout-lab checks are passing. The lab still reproduces the page’s geometry after the root-column alignment change. I’m collecting the complete run’s results before rebuilding with the startup-status correction.


One 2D test still expected an opened directory’s label to stay at its old screen position after unfolding. That conflicts with the accepted arrival repair, which then brings the expanded contents into view. The test’s early motion checks pass; I’m updating its final check to require the label and close control to remain in the frame.

Also completed: uniform directory nodes, historical search/pins/sharing, discovery that survives closing the reader, and the framing, timeline and readability repairs.

**Full gate passed:** 1,529 unit, 113 integration and 254 browser checks. Code committed as `e99d592d`; working tree clean. The live build now carries **all 697 commits**—reload your existing tab when ready.

Screenshots, recording and measurements: [README.md](/workspaces/Live-Documentation/AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-01/remediation/README.md).

One choice remains: the no-entry starting view for repositories like T3. I recommend directory-first Structure; that default remains unchanged pending your answer.

User: This is solid work. I agree with your choice to begin on structural view first if nothing is defined as an entry point, but I also think that we should keep our eyes rather focused on improving that 3D local map view. Now that we are building up our universe in a more exploratory fashion, the experience of exploring in 3D, especially with a relatively small subset of nodes drawn (with one hop away waiting in the wings), honestly still feels very difficult to navigate. And whereas the tuning to resist the effects of zoom on nodes and connectors does help them look sane at most angles, rotating around the full structure is jarring, as the pseudo-orthogonal effects look almost like the universe warping around as I try to rotate around the emergent 3D structure of the visualization. Finally -- and this appears to me to be the largest item that sticks out -- I can't seem to find orphans at all in 3D anymore. Where are they? Where are different microgalaxies that live alongside one another? 

On a simpler/smaller note, I do think that a single click out in the 3D local map should be a "pin" interaction on the clicked node automatically, so that one can quickly pin and unpin elements before switching back over into the higher-detailed 2D Local Map view. 

Over in the 2D local map, we do still occasionally have scenarios where the best possible layout still draws a wire extending out of the right of a node, and walking its way _leftward_ through the screen to reach its destination. It's rare, but it does happen. Next case I find I'll point out. But if we can _detect_ such situations, that's the perfect time for us to highlight or call attention to, visually in some way, the "3D" button. That's truly what the switch from 2D to 3D exists for. And if the switch to 3D fails to make the scene _easier_ to understand, we have uh... y'know... not succeeded. 

This means we've gotta go back to playing around with the forces and equations which define the emergent 3D layout, such that it really can very well spatially optimize and deal with complex scenes which the 2D Local Map was finally starting to struggle with. 

Your efforts are sincerely appreciated, but an enormous amount of common-sense polish remains to be applied, needing only a highly user-minded set of eyes to make the obvious choices (and for those non-obvious choices, of course I am here to help). 

Codex: I’ll make Structure the default when no entry point is declared, and change a direct 3D node click to toggle its pin.

For the larger pass, I’ll trace where disconnected components disappear, examine the zoom compensation during orbit, and compare layouts through actual exploration. I’ll also investigate detecting those backward 2D wires as a reason to highlight the 3D switch. The test is whether switching makes a difficult scene easier to understand.

Hmmm that's fair. Here: if this is easier, try this out first: just make sure that, even if a click does not cause a node to be pinned in the 3D Local Map, that at least a _subsequent click_ on a node can in some way _release_ it from the network (to whatever extent possible; might be depended on by something else, but hopefully you get my point). I just want to be able to add and subtract things from the set of pins I'm trying to look at rather quickly. This comes with a sort of assumption that a set of pins might be a somewhat common form shared with others.

Codex: Understood. A second click should withdraw that node’s own contribution to the exploration; it can remain visible if another retained node still needs it.

I’ll use actual pin/unpin for files, so the resulting set already travels in shared links and into 2D. Directory clicks will toggle their local discovery context, with Open remaining the explicit whole-directory action. That gives us a reversible first version without inventing another kind of directory pin.

## Turn 7 — reversible contributions to the explored network

### Assistant — preceding directory question

"'````text

````

The asynchronous question offered pinning only the directory neighborhood while keeping Open separate, or toggling whole-directory Open/Close. The owner answers with the simpler first experiment below.

### User

````text
>Directory clicks need one explicit choice: today, “Open” retains every file below a directory. Mapping a click directly to that action could unexpectedly reveal an entire repository. I’m asking about that distinction under the “Stop at forks” rule in `AGENTS.md`, while continuing the independent work.

Hmmm that'"'s fair. Here: if this is easier, try this out first: just make sure that, even if a click does not cause a node to be pinned in the 3D Local Map, that at least a _subsequent click_ on a node can in some way _release_ it from the network (to whatever extent possible; might be depended on by something else, but hopefully you get my point). This comes with a sort of assumption that a set of pins might be a somewhat common form shared with others.
"'````

### Assistant

````text
Understood. A second click should withdraw that node’s own contribution to the exploration; it can remain visible if another retained node still needs it.

I’ll use actual pin/unpin for files, so the resulting set already travels in shared links and into 2D. Directory clicks will toggle their local discovery context, with Open remaining the explicit whole-directory action. That gives us a reversible first version without inventing another kind of directory pin.
````

### Accepted first experiment

- Rapid addition/removal of an explored question is the contract; being pinned is not mandatory for every kind of node. Do not force a clicked directory to retain its whole subtree. Files can toggle ordinary shared pins; a directory'"'s temporary local discovery can toggle off on the next click, with the existing explicit Open/Close retaining whole contents.
- Releasing a node must clear its own contributing pin/discovery/subject where applicable, rather than leave it retained accidentally by another state field. Do not suppress a file that another retained question still reaches. Shared pin sets and returning to detailed 2D remain central journeys.
- This steers Turn 6's ongoing task; the no-entry default, rigid orbit, missing islands/orphans, 2D backward-wire cue and force comparisons remain in scope. No new directory-pin schema is needed for this first experiment.
EOF

import { chromium } from '@playwright/test';
import * as fs from 'node:fs/promises';
import { observeGraph, renderedNodes, cameraPose } from '../../tests/e2e/force-graph-observation';

async function main() {
  const phase = process.argv[2] ?? 'baseline';
  const browser = await chromium.launch();
  const context = await browser.newContext({ viewport: { width: 1440, height: 960 }, recordVideo: { dir: '/tmp/live-docs-nov2-videos', size: { width: 1440, height: 960 } } });
  const page = await context.newPage();
  const errors: string[] = [];
  page.on('pageerror', e => errors.push(e.message));
  await page.addInitScript('window.__name = (target) => target');
  await observeGraph(page);
  const frames: unknown[] = [];
  try {
    await page.goto('http://127.0.0.1:8900/samples/estate/?view=local&mode=3d&layout=connections&dir=');
    await page.locator('#graph-svg[aria-busy=\"false\"]').waitFor({timeout:90000});
    await page.mouse.move(20, 20);
    await page.waitForTimeout(1500);
    for (let i=0;i<4;i++) {
      await page.screenshot({path:"'`AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-02/${phase}-estate-orbit-${i}.png`});
      const radii = await page.evaluate(() => {
        const w=window as any, {scene,camera}=w.renderedGraph;const result:any[]=[];
        scene.traverse((mesh:any)=>{if(mesh.userData.spatialBatch?.kind!=='"'nodes')return;for(const entry of mesh.userData.spatialBatch.entries){const m=mesh.matrixWorld.clone();mesh.getMatrixAt(entry.slot,m);result.push({id:entry.data.id,radius:m.elements[0],world:m.elements.slice(12,15)});}});
        return result;
      });
      frames.push({pose:await cameraPose(page),radii});
      await page.mouse.move(1100,720);await page.mouse.down();await page.mouse.move(1250,770,{steps:24});await page.mouse.up();await page.waitForTimeout(1500);
    }
    await fs.writeFile("'`AI-Agent-Workspace/Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/${phase}-rendered-orbit.json`,JSON.stringify({errors,frames},null,2)+'"'\\n');
    console.log(JSON.stringify({phase,errors,nodes:(await renderedNodes(page)).length,url:page.url()}));
  } finally {
    await context.close();
    await page.video()?.saveAs("'`AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-02/${phase}-estate-orbit.webm`);
    await browser.close();
  }
}
void main();
EOF

import * as fs from 'node:fs/promises';
import { spatialReport } from '../../scripts/layout-lab/spatial';
import { findScope, loadBundleGraph } from '../../scripts/layout-lab/scopes';
import { getDefaultTuning } from '../../packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/local-storage';
async function main() {
 const rows:unknown[]=[];
 for(const key of ['estate/five','repository/five']) {
  const run=findScope(key),graph=await loadBundleGraph(run);
  for(const gravity of [.025,.005,.001]) {
   const report=spatialReport(graph,run,{...getDefaultTuning().forceGraph,gravity},3000,{showTests:true,showAssets:false},[],'connections',key.startsWith('estate'));

  }
 }
 await fs.writeFile('AI-Agent-Workspace/Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/gravity-comparison.json',JSON.stringify(rows,null,2)+'\n');

Ran terminal command: "cat >> AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md <<'EOF'

### November 2 implementation checkpoint — reversible clicks and orbit

- Unified sphere, label and keyboard activation. Files toggle actual shared pins; release clears that file's matching discovery, reader and selected-subject contribution, preserving other retained questions. Directories toggle discovery; explicit Open/Close still retains the subtree. Inspect remains in More. Pin borders now identify retained labels.
- Replaced per-object depth compensation with one reference at the actual trackball target. Sphere matrices, surface-trimmed wire endpoints, arrow scale, wire shaders, culling, picking, labels, action strips and framing use that reference. The camera target participates in render invalidation. Build and 43 focused unit checks passed at this checkpoint.
- Private browser evidence: the same estate orbit changed some uploaded sphere radii by 19.74% with the baseline client. With the new reference all uploaded sphere radii and positions remained unchanged across the recorded orbit. Both runs reported no browser errors. These are actual uploaded-buffer measurements; the earlier constructed sample remains separately labeled. Pictures and recordings are under Screenshots/2026-11-02.
- The preview server on 8900 was not actually running after resumption. A first capture failed to connect, and a second exposed a wrong diagnostic helper import; neither is product evidence. The capture was corrected and completed on the existing 8766 static server, then the existing Explore Live Documentation task rebuilt and restarted 8900. No owner page was driven. Do not rely on the preceding checkpoint's claim that the server remained running.
- The pictures exposed anonymous isolated nodes and excessive suppression/dimming of contextual labels in a small scene. The next client names one real member of every disconnected component (isolates included), allows nearby contextual names sooner, and dims names by actual frontier membership instead of merely being unpinned. This is not yet visually reviewed at this checkpoint.
- Added a geometric backward-route detector for ordinary 2D wires, excluding intentional self loops and feedback stubs. It detects reversed endpoints, internal reversed lane runs and overshooting cubic controls. It offers a static outline and contextual tooltip on the 3D button; no flashing callout. Browser review and regression checks remain.
- Existing-force comparison, not a decision: weaker gravity values 0.005 and 0.001 versus 0.025 all settled without overlaps in whole estate (36 files) and the repository five-file discovery report (397 shown). Results are in the November 2 exploration probe. Larger bounds alone do not establish a better layout; rendered comparison remains.

### Assistant — pending design question (verbatim)

"'````text
Connections cannot reach another disconnected group by following wires. I recommend an explicit “Show all files” overview that includes every orphan and group, with a return to the retained local scope. Would you prefer that first, or a group browser for adding selected groups? This is the remaining design fork under AGENTS.md; the click and orbit repairs are continuing independently.

Options: Show all files overview (recommended); Browse and add selected groups.
````

No answer has arrived at this checkpoint. This is a design clarification, not an approval request for the already authorized repairs.
EOF'

update: /workspaces/Live-Documentation/packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/url-state.ts



Lowering gravity alone made the estate overview worse: isolated files moved farther away, forcing the connected work into a smaller, less readable area. I’m keeping that as a rejected experiment.

The first browser regressions have also found interaction failures around keyboard controls and dragging. I’m checking those before treating the new click behavior as finished.

Those failures traced to the new default being applied too broadly: old links naming a specific file were opening its folder tree instead of its connection neighborhood. I’m preserving that existing link behavior. A fresh visit with no requested subject will use Structure when no entry is declared; explicit layouts and shared snapshots keep their choices.

Ran terminal command: cat > AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-02/README.md <<'EOF'
# November 2, 2026 — 3D exploration repairs and comparisons

Historical browser evidence for [Turns 6–7 of the November 1–2 session](../../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-6--november-2-the-3d-exploration-pass). Captured in private Chromium contexts at 1440 × 960. The owner's shared page was not driven. Verification is recorded in the session record; these pictures do not themselves establish a passing test suite.

## Orbit

The whole estate, Connections, default controls, four positions reached by real pointer orbit gestures. The baseline client is "'`b67c3433`; the second run uses the shared orbit-target sizing reference. Layout coordinates were unchanged in both runs. The old draw changed some sphere world radii by 19.74%; the new draw changed none. [Uploaded-buffer measurements](../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/README.md) accompany these pictures. Recordings retain the motion for human review; stills alone do not establish how an animation feels.

| Position | Baseline | Shared reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start | [baseline 0](baseline-estate-orbit-0.png) | [shared 0](shared-target-estate-orbit-0.png) |
| First orbit | [baseline 1](baseline-estate-orbit-1.png) | [shared 1](shared-target-estate-orbit-1.png) |
| Second orbit | [baseline 2](baseline-estate-orbit-2.png) | [shared 2](shared-target-estate-orbit-2.png) |
| Third orbit | [baseline 3](baseline-estate-orbit-3.png) | [shared 3](shared-target-estate-orbit-3.png) |
| Recording | [baseline video](baseline-estate-orbit.webm) | [shared-reference video](shared-target-estate-orbit.webm) |

These early orbit captures precede the label-disclosure repair below.

## Reversible exploration

- [Two independent pins](pins-two-independent.png): GatewayClient and the isolated Estate solution file. Blue label borders identify the retained files. This capture exposed incomplete framing, subsequently corrected so explicit Fit includes every shown node.
- [Orphan released](pins-orphan-released.png): clicking the solution'"'s name removes its pin and sphere, preserving GatewayClient's neighborhood. The same remaining set is portable into 2D.
- [Directory discovered](directory-discovered.png): clicking Contracts reveals its local neighborhood and reader without opening the subtree as a retained directory.
- [Directory released](directory-released.png): a second click withdraws that discovery and reader; the default Structure neighborhood can still contain Contracts. This is not an exclusion filter. The pointer is still over the released directory, so temporary hover emphasis remains.

## Names and gravity comparison

- [Gravity 0.025 with improved names](estate-gravity-0.025.png): the original force model, with names for both isolated files and readable nearby context. The earlier orbit screenshots show the naming problem before repair.
- [Gravity 0.005](estate-gravity-0.005.png): a rejected comparison. More separation alone makes isolated files travel farther and compresses the connected work on screen when fitting everything. The lower gravity is not a selected default. Headless reports for both pictured settings and 0.001 are in the [probe](../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/README.md).

## Backward-wire cue

[Controlled backward arrangement](backward-route-cue-injected.png): a test stylesheet displaces a real consumer card to the left of its providers, and the ordinary packing control redraws the actual routes. Three backward ordinary connections trigger the static outline on 3D. Removing the displacement and restoring the packing clears it. This demonstrates detection and presentation, not reproduction of the owner's still-uncaptured natural case. Self loops and intentional feedback stubs are excluded.
EOF
cat >> AI-Agent-Workspace/Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/README.md <<'EOF'

## Rendered orbit and click checks

The [baseline uploaded buffers](baseline-rendered-orbit.json) and [shared-reference buffers](shared-target-rendered-orbit.json) come from the actual Three batches during four estate camera positions reached by pointer gestures. Baseline maximum radius changes across the three moves were 8.30%, 14.31% and 19.74%; with the shared target each was zero. Every layout coordinate remained unchanged in both runs. Neither page reported a JavaScript error. The [pictures and recordings](../../../Screenshots/2026-11-02/README.md) retain the visible comparison.

[The click check](click-check.json) records that withdrawing Estate.sln's pin removes it while preserving GatewayClient and its dependency neighborhood. This is one journey, not the complete regression suite.

## Existing-force comparison

[The comparison table](gravity-comparison.json) and full coordinates below use the existing force model with gravity 0.025, 0.005 and 0.001. Whole estate has 36 files; the repository five-file discovery report has 397 shown files. All six settled within the 3,000-tick budget with no sphere overlaps. The report uses the layout lab's existing scope semantics, not a claim that its five-file attention is identical to every page entry.

| Scope | 0.025 | 0.005 | 0.001 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Estate, whole | [report](estate-five-gravity-0.025.json) | [report](estate-five-gravity-0.005.json) | [report](estate-five-gravity-0.001.json) |
| Repository, five-file report | [report](repository-five-gravity-0.025.json) | [report](repository-five-gravity-0.005.json) | [report](repository-five-gravity-0.001.json) |

**Visual finding:** simply reducing global gravity is insufficient. In the estate comparison, isolates move farther away and fitting the picture makes connected work smaller. Keep the existing force defaults. Component separation and improving complex connected scenes remain open; no new force model is selected by these measurements.
EOF

const fs=require('fs');const f='packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/local-storage.ts';let s=fs.readFileSync(f,'utf8');s=s.replace('/** Timer handle for debounced UI persistence */\\nexport type PersistUiScheduler = {\\n  schedule: () => void;\\n};\\n\\n/** Timer handle for debounced nav persistence */\\nexport type PersistNavScheduler = {\\n  schedule: () => void;\\n};', "'`/** Debounced persistence with an immediate flush before the page leaves. */
export type PersistUiScheduler = {
  schedule: () => void;
  flush: () => void;
};

/** The same write lifetime for remembered navigation and evidence. */
export type PersistNavScheduler = PersistUiScheduler;

/** Coalesce edits while the page is open; a page exit must not discard the last deliberate choice. */
function delayedWrite(write: () => void): PersistUiScheduler {
  let timer: number | null = null;
  const flush = (): void => {
    if (timer === null) return;
    window.clearTimeout(timer);
    timer = null;
    write();
  };
  return { flush, schedule: (): void => {
    if (timer !== null) window.clearTimeout(timer);
    timer = window.setTimeout(flush, 150);
  } };
}`);
const start=s.indexOf('"'export const createPersistUiScheduler =');
s=s.slice(0,start)+"'`export const createPersistUiScheduler = (
  getState: () => { filters: ExplorerFilters; tuning: TuningConfig }
): PersistUiScheduler => delayedWrite(() => {
  try {
    const payload: PersistedUi = {
      version: PERSISTED_UI_VERSION,
      ...changedFromDefaults(getState(), { filters: getDefaultFilters(), tuning: getDefaultTuning() })
    };
    window.localStorage.setItem(PERSISTED_UI_KEY, JSON.stringify(payload));
  } catch {
    // Storage may be unavailable or blocked.
  }
});

/**
 * Coalesce remembered navigation writes for 150ms, with a flush for page exit.
 * Inspecting a different file never replaces the remembered subject.
 */
export const createPersistNavScheduler = (
  getState: () => { view: ViewName; perspective: LocalMapPerspective; spatialLayout: SpatialLayout; openDirectories: ReadonlySet<string>; selectedNodeId: string | null; inspection: InspectionTarget | null; discovery?: DiscoveryTarget | null }
): PersistNavScheduler => delayedWrite(() => {
  try {
    const state = getState();
    const payload: PersistedNavV1 = {
      version: PERSISTED_NAV_VERSION,
      view: state.view,
      perspective: state.perspective,
      spatialLayout: state.spatialLayout,
      openDirectories: [...state.openDirectories],
      nodeId: state.selectedNodeId,
      inspection: readPersistedInspection(state.inspection),
      ...(state.discovery !== undefined ? { discovery: readPersistedDiscovery(state.discovery) } : {})
    };
    window.localStorage.setItem(PERSISTED_NAV_KEY, JSON.stringify(payload));
  } catch {
    // Storage may be unavailable or blocked.
  }
});
`;
fs.writeFileSync(f,s);
NODE'

import * as fs from 'node:fs/promises';
import { spatialReport } from '../../scripts/layout-lab/spatial';
import { findScope, loadBundleGraph } from '../../scripts/layout-lab/scopes';
import { getDefaultTuning } from '../../packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/local-storage';
function groupsOf(report: ReturnType<typeof spatialReport>){
 const beside=new Map(report.nodes.map(n=>[n.id,new Set<string>()]));for(const e of report.links)if(e.pulls){beside.get(e.source)"'!.add(e.target);beside.get(e.target)!.add(e.source);}
 const nodes=new Map(report.nodes.map(n=>[n.id,n])), unseen=new Set(nodes.keys()), groups=[];
 for(const id of nodes.keys()){if(!unseen.delete(id))continue;const ids=[id];for(let i=0;i<ids.length;i++)for(const v of beside.get(ids[i])!)if(unseen.delete(v))ids.push(v);const members=ids.map(id=>nodes.get(id)!);const mean={x:0,y:0,z:0};for(const p of members){mean.x+=p.x/members.length;mean.y+=p.y/members.length;mean.z+=p.z/members.length;}
 groups.push({files:members.length,seed:ids[0],mean,radius:Math.max(...members.map(p=>Math.hypot(p.x-mean.x,p.y-mean.y,p.z-mean.z)+p.radius))});}
 let overlaps=0,minClearance=Infinity;for(let i=0;i<groups.length;i++)for(let j=i+1;j<groups.length;j++){const a=groups[i],b=groups[j];const clearance=Math.hypot(a.mean.x-b.mean.x,a.mean.y-b.mean.y,a.mean.z-b.mean.z)-a.radius-b.radius;minClearance=Math.min(minClearance,clearance);if(clearance<0)overlaps++;}
 return {groups,overlappingComponentBounds:overlaps,minClearance};
}
async function main(){const rows=[];for(const key of ['"'estate/five','repository/five']){const run=findScope(key),graph=await loadBundleGraph(run);for(const componentSeparation of [0,.5,.8]){const report=spatialReport(graph,run,{...getDefaultTuning().forceGraph,componentSeparation},3000,{showTests:true,showAssets:false},[],'connections',true);const metrics=groupsOf(report);const row={bundle:run.bundle,componentSeparation,files:report.nodes.length,ticks:report.ticks,still:report.still,bodyOverlaps:report.overlaps,bounds:report.bounds,...metrics};rows.push(row);console.log(JSON.stringify({...row,groups:metrics.groups.length}));await fs.writeFile("'`AI-Agent-Workspace/Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/${run.bundle}-component-separation-${componentSeparation}.json`,JSON.stringify(report)+'"'\n');}}
 await fs.writeFile('AI-Agent-Workspace/Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/component-comparison.json',JSON.stringify(rows,null,2)+'\n');}
void main();
EOF

Ran terminal command: cat >> AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md <<'EOF'

### Turn 8 implementation checkpoint — overview entrance and component-force trial

- Added a Show all files / Return to local scope action beside the shown-file count. It uses the existing scan-root Open/Close entry and preserves independent file pins and directory openings. It explicitly pauses history and Fits the resulting scope. It is implemented, not yet fully journey-tested.
- Added a trial Group Separation control (default **0**, not selected yet). It reduces only the gravity of each positive-spring connected component's centroid toward the common origin, preserving the component's internal gravitational force. No component centers are hand-placed; no synthetic nodes, hidden springs or excluded facts are introduced. The browser and headless layout use the same field. The force remains conservative with exact repulsion, and gravity's old stiffness bound remains valid. Springs now reject invalid sphere indices before component construction.
- Focused physics/layout/settings/help run: 70 tests passed, including numerical energy-gradient checks on disconnected components, unchanged relative internal forces, isolated/slack-link behavior and parameter validation. Two help inventory assertions expected 46 controls; the new control makes 47. Updated that exact inventory count; no threshold or behavioral assertion relaxed. Application build passed.
- Whole-picture headless comparison at separation 0 / 0.5 / 0.8: estate (36 files, 4 components) settles in 264 / 282 / 176 ticks, zero body overlaps in each; overlapping component bounding spheres fall from 1 to 0 to 0. Repository (913 eligible files, 94 components) settles in 602 / 462 / 250 ticks, zero body overlaps; overlapping component bounding spheres fall from 63 to 0 to 0. These bounds are a conservative diagnostic, not the visual objective. Complete positions/settings and comparison metrics are under the November 2 exploration probe. The browser comparison is next; no new default is selected by these numbers.
- Private estate component pictures are running as terminal **20021 placeholder — check actual tool session on resumption**; replace this marker with the returned id. No full test gate is running. The third gate was intentionally interrupted for broader implementation after the new user instruction.
EOF

Ran terminal command: cat >> AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md <<'EOF'

- The slow whole-repository browser has no reported JavaScript errors. Actual observations at roughly 10/20/30 seconds: 44/77/109 rendered frames; 769/913/913 submitted nodes; still moving throughout. Its first pictures show the original dense central cloud. This is real slow drawing/motion, not a failed selector or a hidden view. Profiling the moderate-separation candidate's browser CPU before choosing a remedy; the private run records its own scene/counters, not agent traces.
- Began the transition repair: the application now enters the native correspondence animation even without a selected file, retaining an independent pin set's semantics. Closed directory boxes join the captured cards with their real directory identities, so they can travel into Structure hubs; directory-only shells can animate too. The newest pin may supply the transition's named token without becoming the selected subject. Still to playtest in motion and write regressions afterward.
- Direct-click labels now describe the actual Pin/Unpin or discovery-release action, and their pressed state reflects the contribution the click withdraws. The explicit Pin button retains an inspected discovery as intended. Moved the overview action beside, rather than inside, the inventory text so the count remains a plain count.
EOF

The large-scene slowdown appears mostly outside the JavaScript physics work in the first CPU sample. I’m separating label painting from canvas drawing next so I can fix the actual bottleneck.

Tool execution failed: Exit code 143



The larger candidate is now visibly separating into distinct networks instead of one central tangle. I’m keeping the moderate setting under review; the stronger one wastes too much space.

I also found why directory transitions can lose continuity: the gradual reveal may initially draw only one hub, so the folders visible in 2D have no counterparts yet. I’m making those existing identities enter before the transition, without adding pins or enlarging the requested scope.

### Later Turn 8 checkpoint — coherent entry, physics and transition behavior

- Selected Group Separation **0.5** as the root's implementation judgment under Turn 8, after the estate comparison and the larger scene's distinct emerging networks. This is not recorded as an owner-selected numeric value. The stronger 0.8 spends excessive screen space on separation. Explicit zero remains available. No hand-placed component centers or hidden graph exclusions.
- The whole-repository browser's native graphics readback/compositing dominates the diagnostic trace. Over five-second comparison windows, normal / labels hidden / canvas hidden / normal again drew 9 / 10 / 123 / 11 frames. Hiding labels is not a fix; those were private diagnostic mutations only. The raw browser execution trace is temporary under "'`/tmp`; the retained probe has conditions and aggregate results. Testing an opaque canvas context (the canvas is already cleared opaque) without disabling antialiasing or changing geometry; no performance claim yet.
- Root-opening headless parity needed care: the earlier whole-graph report asked about every file directly, while the page reveals a directory'"'s member set. A separate member-reveal measurement settled at 421 ticks for separation zero and 458 for 0.5. The first two-case run was terminated with exit 143 after saving zero; the isolated bounded 0.5 retry completed. Do not describe that interrupted run as a numerical failure or a success.
- Corrected the layout lab's report to ask directly about pins, rather than their already-retained neighbors (which accidentally added a hop), and to reveal a whole root as members. Added a meaningful toy-chain/isolated-file regression in "'`scripts/layout-lab/spatial.test.ts`, staged so generation can see it. Earlier probe results remain historical measurements with their original initial conditions.
- New transition counterpart priority reveals only already-in-scope 2D file/folder identities before the handoff; it neither adds pins nor changes logical scope. Added a pure membership/order regression. Directory-only and pin-only scopes now enter the animation. Playtesting of these latest changes remains.
- Names of larger components take precedence over isolated-file names when they compete for space; declared starting files now receive explicit name priority. New/changed name measurements are batched after DOM writes, with viewport-width-sensitive caching, avoiding one layout flush per new label. No node or wire is removed.
- A real usability fault in sphere clicks: the renderer treated any pointer movement as a drag, whereas the view allowed six pixels of click motion. Shared that six-pixel slop and begin dragging only past it, so ordinary hand jitter does not cancel Pin. Requires a browser regression after looking.
- Focused model/scope run: 97 tests passed, one existing re-easing stability assertion failed (maximum movement 4.5723 versus allowed 2.6007). The new weaker component mode exposed anchoring on a single quiet oscillation turning point. Changed anchoring to require the existing ten consecutive quiet ticks; kept both movement thresholds intact. Reset that count when membership/forces change. The same low-level physics test harness now follows that controller policy. A focused rerun is active; do not claim it passed yet.
EOF'

The first separation default failed an existing stability check: easing an unchanged picture again moved it too far. A stricter anchoring rule made other layouts fail to settle, so I’m backing that experiment out.

I’m comparing milder separation settings against both readability and stability. Separately, I found a genuine orphan bug: a single node had a zero-size settling reference because the calculation ignored its radius. That correction stays.

### Current Turn 8 state — milder default and completed directory journey

- Revised the selected Group Separation default to **0.2** after comparing 0, 0.2, 0.25, 0.3, 0.35, 0.4, 0.45 and 0.5 against re-easing stability and the estate. The preserved one-percent stability check passes at 0.2 (0.936% movement in the recorded sweep), while 0.25–0.5 move too far. This is the agent's balance of separation and orientation, not an owner-selected number. The stronger candidates remain dated evidence.
- Rejected and removed the ten-tick pre-anchor and residual-force anchoring experiments. The residual-force version made approximate fields fail to settle and distorted the established gravity-off comparison. Neither movement threshold was relaxed. Kept two independent corrections: a cold picture cannot anchor before its requested reveal is complete, and settling uses sphere extents so a lone file has a nonzero size reference.
- The 0.2 model/scope run passed 98 of 99 checks; the remaining hand-drag check used an arbitrary +X displacement which, after a layout change, scarcely stretched the tested spring. Changed the stimulus to stretch the actual spring outward by half its settled length, preserving both assertions (partner moves more than 10 units and the resulting pair distance stays within ten percent). The focused layout file then passed all 26 tests. This is a test setup correction, not a weaker distance gate; the old fixed-axis probe's historical numbers do not describe the new stimulus.
- Completed a nine-state estate journey with no browser errors: Portal's 2D directory, its correspondence animation into Structure, Connections, whole overview, an isolated Estate.sln pin, return to the retained local scope, animation back to 2D, and release of the orphan. Selected subject remained null; Portal remained independently open; the scan-root opening was added and removed without losing either. Captures "'`journey-01` through `journey-09`, `directory-journey.webm`, and `directory-journey.json` retain the evidence. The temporary floating folder names in the reverse Connections transition were identified and repaired: unmatched folders fade as full boxes, without an invented sphere/name token. Transition names now remain at 13px.
- Name buttons now capture a pointer press so a moving name acts on the identity pressed, and cancel activation after deliberate movement beyond the same six-pixel slop. Keyboard activation still uses the current name. The earlier opaque-canvas trial was removed: the installed Three renderer requests an alpha-capable context regardless of that option, and no performance benefit was established. The actual private renderer reports Vulkan SwiftShader; the native readback bottleneck is a measured software-renderer limitation, not evidence that the JavaScript field is blocking for those durations.
- Added bounded directory discovery in Connections from the same pruned containment neighborhood, and resolved directory aliases collapsed into their sole child hub. A deeper-only neighborhood offers an explicit Open action instead of becoming unexplained black space or opening a whole repository implicitly. Explicit file discovery is retained even when its archetype is normally filtered. Empty hints respect reader/history obstructions. These latest scope changes passed 19 focused scope/reveal tests and an application build; final browser checks remain.
- Directory action validity is now indexed once from current file paths instead of scanning the entire repository for every named hub on every frame. Context labels acknowledge temporary directory discovery and historical names. A final focused click/discovery browser check is pending; check terminal 40972 for its actual result.
- No full gate is running. Three prior full-gate attempts are historical: the first stopped on test cleanup typing, the second on the one-sentence help rule, the third was deliberately interrupted for the broader Turn 8 implementation. A full gate is mandatory after the remaining journey checks and final documentation.
EOF'

### Large-directory findings and final scope controls

- T3 now begins with 118 files and meaningful directory landmarks. Ordinary clicks followed apps → server → src → mcp, reaching the OrchestratorMcpService name from the reader instead of requiring prior knowledge. The server discovery did not become idle within 60 seconds in this software-rendered recording, although further input worked; the capture is explicitly still moving, not a final settle claim. The 805-file source neighborhood and 1,242-file MCP Connections neighborhood are too dense to read as overviews. Selecting the visible service narrows it to 441 files. This is a remaining usability finding, not concealed by the good estate result.
- Added direct access to the existing Hops Shown setting beside the 3D layout controls, retaining the owner's default of two and the existing range. Reducing context should be normal navigation rather than a hunt through Tuning. It shares the same saved/exported state, pauses playback and Fits the resulting scope; a path disables it because that picture has an explicit route. Directory discovery now requests Fit as its neighborhood unfolds, avoiding the server view's clipped new context. Files retain the existing conditional evidence-framing behavior.
- The first five new browser cases finished with four passes; the directory case's final assertion expected null instead of the compressed address's omitted discovery field. Corrected that assertion to undefined; the prior framing and state assertions passed. A rerun and the full gate remain.
- Review caught a mistake in this turn's layout-lab test editing: "'`spatial.test.ts` already existed, despite the earlier note calling it new. The two new parity cases had overwritten three older factual/filter/Structure cases. Restored all three existing checks unchanged and retained the two additions in their own fixture scope; all five pass. No coverage removal is accepted. Corrected the authored notes instead of replacing the file'"'s real October 13 origin.
- Delivered commentary: “T3 now starts with readable directory landmarks instead of the 9,341-file knot. I can enter its application directories without knowing a service name first. Four of the five new browser checks passed; the fifth reached the correct released state but expected "'`null` where the URL decoder uses an absent value. I’ve corrected that assertion.”
- Delivered commentary: “The large-directory trial exposed another real weakness: switching T3’s MCP directory to Connections expands to 1,242 files and becomes a white knot. Reducing the hop count helps, but that control is buried in Tuning. I’m bringing it onto the 3D toolbar so narrowing the neighborhood is an ordinary navigation action. I’ll keep the existing two-hop default.”
EOF

## Components and the complete directory journey

The [Turn 8 continuation](../../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-8--continue-until-the-directory-exploration-journey-is-ready-for-new-users) compares component-centroid gravity without inventing centers or hiding relationships. [Zero](estate-components-0.png), [0.5](estate-components-0.5.png) and [0.8](estate-components-0.8.png) are trials, **not the selected default**. The final default is 0.2: stronger separation used excessive screen space or failed the existing re-easing stability contract. [The probe](../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/README.md) preserves the measurements and rejected experiments.

The repository's [10-second](repository-diagnostic-0.png), [20-second](repository-diagnostic-1.png) and [30-second](repository-diagnostic-2.png) diagnostics use separation zero and are still moving. The [0.5 early](repository-components-0.5-growing.png) and [later](repository-components-0.5-later.png) captures show the stronger trial's emerging components, not a final idle picture. Software rendering dominates this environment's frame time; these are not claims of fluid large-scene animation.

The nine-state estate journey uses the selected 0.2 and preserves Portal independently of the root overview and isolated file. No selected file is required for either perspective transition. [The recording](directory-journey.webm) retains the full gesture sequence; [its states and renderer](../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/directory-journey.json) report no page errors. These captures precede the later toolbar hop selector and pin-only anchor repair.

| Capture | What it shows |
| --- | --- |
| [01 — directory in 2D](journey-01-directory-2d.png) | Portal retained, its direct files and closed subdirectories. |
| [02 — correspondence](journey-02-directory-transition.png) | Closed folders become their actual Structure hubs. |
| [03 — Structure](journey-03-directory-structure.png) | The directory neighborhood, no selected subject. |
| [04 — Connections](journey-04-directory-connections.png) | Same retained directory, references instead of containment. |
| [05 — whole overview](journey-05-whole-overview.png) | All 36 files, including the isolated solution file, and a quieter reference web. |
| [06 — local scope plus orphan](journey-06-local-with-orphan-pin.png) | Root opening withdrawn; Portal and the independent orphan remain. |
| [07 — reverse transition](journey-07-return-transition.png) | Unmatched closed folders fade as complete boxes, without phantom name tokens. |
| [08 — retained 2D](journey-08-retained-2d.png) | Portal plus the solution file carried into the detailed representation. |
| [09 — orphan released](journey-09-orphan-released.png) | Second click removes only that pin's contribution. |

[One isolated file](single-orphan.png) settles at a useful size. [Temporary Portal discovery in Connections](discovered-directory-connections.png) shows sixteen files beside the reader without retaining its subtree. [The actual pointer check](../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/direct-interaction-check.json) covers small hand jitter and a name moved between press and release.

## T3: discover, narrow, retain, change perspective

The canonical T3 analysis is unchanged: 28,145 files and 81,634 edges. Only its Explorer client was rebuilt. The [first journey record](../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/t3-first-journey.json) separates useful navigation from the dense scenes it exposed. [The recording](t3-directory-journey.webm) includes those problems, pauses and continuing motion; it is not a curated success reel.

| [Apps](t3-02-apps-discovery.png) | Five discoverable application directories and their reader. |
| [Server](t3-03-server-discovery.png) | Still-moving 100-file discovery, with clipped context; this prompted Fit on directory discovery. |

| [Service retained in 2D](t3-09-service-retained-2d.png) | Latest repeat of the pin-only handoff. |
| [Return to 3D](t3-10-service-return-3d.png) | Same retained service and one-hop neighborhood. |

The [final journey state](../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/t3-final-journey.json) records pins, settings, actual submitted files and page errors. Neither this trial nor the estate turns a thousand-file picture into an explanation: directory discovery and explicit context reduction are the useful entrances.

## Component-centroid gravity trials and selected balance

[The comparison](component-comparison.json) measures zero, 0.5 and 0.8 separation of component centroids from origin gravity, leaving internal gravity unchanged. Full reports retain [estate zero](estate-component-separation-0.json), [estate 0.5](estate-component-separation-0.5.json), [estate 0.8](estate-component-separation-0.8.json), [repository zero](repository-component-separation-0.json), [repository 0.5](repository-component-separation-0.5.json) and [repository 0.8](repository-component-separation-0.8.json). All settle with no body overlaps; enclosing component spheres are a conservative diagnostic, not the visual goal. These early whole-scope runs asked about every file directly, rather than revealing the root's files as members.

The corrected member-reveal runs are [zero](repository-members-0.json) and [0.5](repository-members-0.5.json): 421 and 458 ticks. [The single-case retry summary](members-comparison.json) contains 0.5; the earlier combined run saved zero before exit 143 and is not claimed to have finished both. The report now starts hops at actual pins, without the accidental extra hop through their retained neighborhood.

[The stability sweep](separation-stability.json) checks how far a settled picture moves when eased again. The selected default is **0.2**, an agent judgment under the owner's November 2 continuation grant. Its recorded 0.936% move satisfies the existing one-percent contract; 0.25–0.5 exceed it. 0.5 and 0.8 are rejected defaults despite stronger separation. Ten-quiet-tick and residual-force anchoring experiments were rejected too; they are not in the final implementation. Kept corrections require completed reveal before cold anchoring and include sphere extents in the settling reference, including a lone orphan.

## Browser cost and end-user journeys

[The browser profile](repository-browser-profile.json) and [frame comparison](repository-frame-cost.json) identify native canvas readback/compositing as the dominant cost in this SwiftShader environment. Over equal five-second windows, normal / names hidden / canvas hidden / normal drew 9 / 10 / 123 / 11 frames. Hiding either layer was diagnostic only; neither was adopted. An opaque-canvas option experiment was removed because Three still requested an alpha-capable context and no improvement was established. Temporary raw profiling traces were not product outputs.

[The estate journey](directory-journey.json) preserves independent Portal retention through overview, orphan pinning, local return, both perspective transitions and release. [The direct interaction check](direct-interaction-check.json) verifies small pointer jitter and a name moved between press and release. [The first T3 journey](t3-first-journey.json) records the new directory-first entrance and the dense scenes it exposed; [the repeated T3 journey](t3-final-journey.json) records explicit hop reduction, a discovered service and its shared pin across 2D/3D. [Pictures and recordings](../../../Screenshots/2026-11-02/README.md) name which states are trials, still moving or superseded. Final test outcomes belong to the root session record, not these measurements.

The import fixes pass lint, and the complete run has restarted. I’m keeping runtime code unchanged during verification. The live-browser findings and rejected trials are preserved alongside the final captures, including the large scenes that still need narrowing.

The restarted run has completed documentation generation and validation. The six focused browser journeys remain green; I’m waiting for the full lint/build/test chain before calling this ready. The owner’s shared browser tab is still untouched.

Lint is clean, and the full build is progressing. The main behavioral changes are now fixed in place for testing: reversible contributions, stable orbit sizing, discoverable whole-directory scope, and a visible way to reduce context when a large network becomes unreadable.

All 1,541 unit tests passed, and the editor reports no errors. Integration tests are running next, followed by the documentation audits and the complete browser suite.

The build, all 1,541 unit tests, all 113 integration tests and the documentation audits passed. The browser-test bundles are rebuilding now. The main preview already includes the new client and all 697 currently committed history steps.

The 265-case browser run is underway. Its initial citation, navigation and Back/Forward checks pass. I’m letting it cover the wider interactions before committing, particularly history, pathfinding, framing and keyboard behavior.

The directory, preset-framing and declared-entry discovery checks pass in the full run. That includes releasing an opened directory through the shared tray and preserving the chosen directory frame when a file is also selected.

Discovery, reader closure and shared-link restoration pass in the full suite. The new hop-control and whole-overview tests also pass there, including finding an isolated file and returning to the earlier pins and directory. No browser failures so far.

The direct pin/release, directory-release, drag, touch and keyboard checks pass. Manual pan also survives a 2D/3D round trip without restarting the settled layout. The suite is now checking the remaining camera and rendering behavior.

One browser check has failed on an outdated count: it expects fewer than 36 files, but now counts Structure’s folder hubs too—37 total nodes, of which 19 are files. I’m correcting it to count files while retaining the framing assertions for every node. The rest of the suite is continuing so I can collect any other failures together.

The reveal and placement checks are passing, including keeping existing spheres fixed when new files are placed without simulation. The only browser failure so far is the file-versus-folder count assertion, which is now corrected in the test.

The orbit and wire-rendering checks all pass, including actual GPU pixels at both display scales. Orbit preserves the uploaded sphere sizes and wire endpoints, while zoom resistance, clipping and tapered wires retain their intended behavior.

History checks are passing too: resize-only changes, stable identities through renames, investigation of deleted files, and commit/range navigation. The new exploration work has not broken those earlier repairs.

The suite found a real interaction bug: the expanded pin tray’s invisible layout box can intercept clicks on History, even though the visible panel sits above it. I’m making that empty space ignore pointer input while keeping the panel and its buttons interactive. I’ll verify the same historical-file journey after the current run finishes.

The 2D branch and perspective checks are passing, including subject anchoring, independent symbol pins, pruning, directory boundaries and cross-connections. I’m still collecting the full run’s results before rebuilding for the two corrections.

Pathfinding and keyboard recovery pass in both perspectives. Another failure has appeared in the hidden-view pinning check; I’m inspecting its trace while the remaining tests continue.

That hidden-view failure was an old test setup: it clicked a name expecting inspection, while the intended action is now Pin. I’ve changed the setup to More → Inspect and kept all pause/resume assertions. The retention and directory-pruning checks continue to pass.

The no-network and content-policy checks pass across the main, estate and history bundles. The browser run has reached the perspective and sharing checks, with the same three identified failures and no additional ones so far.

Search, sharing and the legend checks pass. The suite is working through its larger fixed-scene layout audits now; those take longer than the small journey tests. No further failures have appeared.

The larger repository scenes pass in both 2D and 3D, including the retained five-file and chain layouts. The run still has only the three already diagnosed failures.

import {chromium} from '@playwright/test';
import * as fs from 'node:fs/promises';
import {compressSnapshot,DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT} from '../../packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/compressed-url-state';
async function main(){const browser=await chromium.launch(),page=await browser.newPage({viewport:{width:1400,height:900}});const errors:string[]=[];page.on('pageerror',e=>errors.push(e.message));try{
await page.goto('http://127.0.0.1:8900/samples/history/?s='+encodeURIComponent(compressSnapshot({...DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT,perspective:'3d',spatialLayout:'structure',layout:{forceGraph:{updateEnergy:0,hops:4}}})));
await page.locator('#graph-svg[aria-busy="false"]').waitFor();await page.getByRole('button',{name:'History',exact:true}).click();await page.locator('.history-tick[data-step="3"]').click();await page.locator('#search-subject').fill('legacy');await page.locator('#search-subject-results .search-row--file').click();await page.locator('#graph-svg[aria-busy="false"]').waitFor();await page.getByRole('button',{name:'Show pins',exact:true}).click();await page.mouse.move(4,4);await page.screenshot({path:'AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-02/history-and-open-pins.png'});
const button=page.getByRole('button',{name:'History',exact:true});const hit=await button.evaluate(element=>{const r=element.getBoundingClientRect();return document.elementFromPoint(r.x+r.width/2,r.y+r.height/2)?.closest('button')?.getAttribute('aria-label');});await button.click();const closed=await page.locator('.history-scrubber').isHidden();await page.screenshot({path:'AI-Agent-Workspace/Screenshots/2026-11-02/history-closed-with-open-pins.png'});await fs.writeFile('AI-Agent-Workspace/Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/tray-hit-target.json',JSON.stringify({hit,historyClosed:closed,pinTrayOpen:await page.locator('.pin-tray-panel').isVisible(),errors},null,2)+'\n');console.log({hit,closed,errors});if(hit"'!=='"'History'||"'!closed||errors.length)throw Error('"'History control failed with open pin tray');
}finally{await browser.close();}}void main();
EOF
