# November 1–2, 2026 — an end-user review, firefly history and reversible 3D exploration

Historical session summary. Harness: Codex in VS Code; the owner addresses the root as Astra. No subagents were used. Summary author: the root Codex agent, November 2, 2026, at the owner's explicit close-out request. Complete for the two available captures, both read in full, through the closing request at Turn 9. This summary's eventual delivery reply is not yet a captured source.

## Sources and coverage

- [Agent-maintained root record](../2026-11-01.1.record.md): nine owner turns, the delivered replies to Turns 1, 5 and 8, intervening root responses and progress updates, and authored work/verification notes. The closing read covered all 170,951 characters present after Turn 9 was appended; later closing notes record the archival work itself. Historical resumption blocks and intermediate candidates remain as dated evidence; the current Resumption and later outcomes supersede them.
- [Supplementary visible export](../2026-11-01.export.md), preserved under its received filename, **313,392 bytes**, SHA-256 **"'`32c7b43c4f9fd5910f7f206414359517ccb68250b42440a051dfc5a123ec1509`**. Read all 312,849 characters in contiguous bounded slices. It begins with Turn 1 and ends immediately after Turn 7'"'s user clarification at an empty "'`Codex:` prefix. It includes visible tool-invocation text and additional root commentary, but not all tool results; several linked labels became inline code without their destinations. Some progress messages are grouped after tool activity, so their placement supplies no exact timestamp. It does **not** capture Turn 7'"'s reply, Turn 8's work or Turn 9. No broader native-export coverage is asserted.
- [November inventory](../README.md) joins the captures and summary. Neither capture was reconstructed from hidden reasoning, and no mixed agent trace or subagent thread was extracted. The raw export remains unchanged.
- Git verifies the five pre-close-out commits below. Screenshots, playback measurements, layout reports and browser traces describe the software; they do not replace the conversation sources. The final complete test results are recorded in the root record and implementation commit messages; this archival pass does not rerun application tests.

## Context

The October 31 work had made 3D discovery the default, brought history playback speed beside Play and introduced changed-file emphasis. The owner wanted another opinionated review like the successful October exercise, using the whole October story and the complete history rather than a recent sample. The session became two implementation rounds: first historical investigation, framing and the owner's live firefly correction; then a broader 3D directory/pin journey after the owner found orbit distortion, missing orphans and awkward navigation. The code landed only after full gates, with unsuccessful trials and the remaining limits retained alongside the accepted pictures.

## Turn 1

The owner asked Astra to read every October non-summary "'`*.record.md`, rebuild the latest Fable work and explore it as an end user: “tell me where it sucks for you as an end user.” This explicitly replaced the ordinary five-summary bootstrap. The root read all **27 October records**, about three megabytes, with contiguous reading checkpoints, as well as the standing guidance, Memory and vision. The preceding session was already summarized. The initial task authorized review, not implementation.

The root rebuilt this repository, the small C# estate and the existing T3 analysis'"'s Explorer client. It reconstructed and actually played the complete **694-commit** timeline, then investigated particular changes. The review's strongest findings were concrete journeys:

- Immediately before the January 12 GraphStore removal, historical search returned nothing for "'`graphStore`, despite the displayed graph containing it. The next step reported 58 deleted files and 824 removed wires; deleted rows were not actionable.
- Inspecting core expanded the generator neighborhood from 124 to 168 files behind the reader, then closing the reader discarded the expansion.
- Opening Portal at an ordinary 1280×800 put content outside the frame until Reset Zoom; independent root columns also formed an unnecessary staircase.
- T3'"'s fallback start showed a 9,341-file knot. A service name already known from the archive reduced it to 441; one hop reduced it to 33. That was useful after tuning, but a poor entrance for a stranger.
- Full-history growth could leave the camera inside the graph. Long quiet periods squeezed recent commits together; changed or removed identities were hard to investigate from the fast panel.
- Oversized recursive Structure spheres hid their descendants, while some evidence labels and the visible-scope indication still needed work.

Search ranking, reverse-path recovery, the hideable history panel, nearby speed control and inventory counts survived actual use. The root initially preferred Dim to Glow in still comparisons. It separated hidden-browser/preview-port failures from product faults and rejected a prematurely captured Fit image as evidence. The [review](../../../../Screenshots/2026-11-01/README.md), with 25 pictures and reproductions, landed as "'`72601209`; no product source changed in that commit. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-1); [delivered review](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-2).

## Turn 2

The owner accepted the critiques: “we want to be able to look at directories and understand their contents well, especially if those contents have interconnectivity of some kind,” and “Go ahead and tackle the pain points as you see them.” They also returned to the unfinished directory-sizing pass. Their exact wording was that “we still have differentially-sized directory nodes.” The agent initially called this an explicit reiteration of uniform sizing; that attribution was corrected later. The implemented uniform junctions follow the earlier October 28 uniform-size request and the accepted review, rather than replacing the November 1 sentence with wording it did not contain.

The root used the repository'"'s Live Documentation skill to trace the affected modules, then built the first repair set:

- Uniform directory volume, with file symbol sizing retained and root/local names prioritized.
- Directory unfolding followed by the minimum movement into the usable 2D frame, including the floating controls; independent root columns aligned without introducing false membrane constraints.
- Historical search over the displayed moment, actionable deletions that open the preceding moment, and portable lifetime identities for past-only files. Existing engine rename/lifetime traversal distinguishes an old file from a later file at the same path. Historical lookup nodes stay outside today's canonical graph. Readers show the counts and connections known to history and explicitly lack old prose and symbol names.
- An addressable discovery target independent of the reader's visibility. Closing evidence preserves the discovered context; new exploration or Clear can withdraw it. The requested neighborhood is framed beside overlays without changing the canvas's dimensions.
- Commit/date/message search, adjacent-step controls, an inclusive timeline range that preserves the complete sequence, and a requested camera frame that follows historical growth until a manual gesture takes over.
- Readable symbol names, corrected authored provenance, directory contrast and a scope footer that remains visible in a short viewport.

One real design fork stayed open: without declared entries, should a fresh 3D visit begin with Structure or a bounded neighborhood of the most connected file? The root recommended Structure, asked explicitly, and kept the old default while independent work continued.

Browser use and the gates found additional causes: a self-resizing timeline observer was replaced by CSS container sizing; a fresh page returning Back to a historical pin had scrubbed it before loading its sequence; the refactor lost plain "'`?dir=` openings; lower cards could fit the canvas but sit behind permanent controls. Completely new neighborhoods were being tethered to arbitrary seed positions: the 11→191-file Clear reproduction took 1,504 headless ticks held versus 322 unfolded. New neighborhoods now unfold, known positions remain held, and empty requested scopes are idle. Incorrect test premises were corrected explicitly, including the newly shorter root picture and the camera'"'s uncovered-frame centre; their tolerances remained intact.

These repairs continued through the live steering of Turns 3–5 and landed together as "'`e99d592d`. This turn'"'s initial Dim-default preference was superseded below. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-2).

## Turn 3

While using the live build, the owner reported brightness **ramping in**, then suddenly dipping at each new commit. They wanted a changed node to “simply glows white for a turn,” returning to its ordinary color at the next commit; changed wires could become more visible, and coupling should continue to affect spatial length.

The root confirmed the build was current, but initially said the within-step fade could make dimmed nodes “appear to brighten.” It promised immediate white held for the whole commit and verification at both **1 and 0.5 step/s**. The owner was actively using the shared page; disruptive checks moved to private contexts and their page was left alone. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-3).

## Turn 4

The owner rejected the perceptual explanation: the increase was unambiguous, and a human eye could see temporal subtleties that the model's stills did not establish. The root corrected itself. Dim used "'`alpha × (1 − 0.8 × strength)` while strength fell from one to zero, so unaffected nodes really rose from 20% to 100% opacity before resetting. “I described that too weakly.”

The running fourth gate was deliberately interrupted for this correction, not counted as success. The fix removed the animation clock and play-state plumbing from emphasis instead of reversing another fade. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-4).

## Turn 5

The owner supplied the governing image: “a network of occasionally firing fireflies. Try to maximize surprise.” Sudden global dim-and-undim was awful; dimming itself could still be explored.

The final treatment is a steady network with sharp local events. Glow is the default again: changed nodes are exactly white for the displayed commit, then return to their base color unless changed again. Optional Dim/Both remain steady within the interval. The final commit can be emphasized over today'"'s graph while history is open; closing history restores normal appearance. Random or decorative flashes were never authorized.

The private probe sampled actual uploaded RGB and alpha at three times within each interval, at both requested speeds, then across the next commit, final commit and closing history. Those values stayed constant within the interval. [Pictures, recording and measurements](../../../../Screenshots/2026-11-01/remediation/README.md) preserve what was checked; stills alone were not treated as motion evidence.

The first round's fifth complete run finished 252/254 browser cases. Investigating its startup-status failure first led to a proposed test exception, then to the actual cause: after adding nodes, queued simulation work was busy while "'`aria-busy` stayed idle until the next frame. The source status was synchronized and the **original stricter test restored unchanged**. The other failure expected the final directory label to remain at its old position despite the accepted post-unfolding frame recovery; the early anchor bounds stayed, and the final check required the label and Close action in frame. The footer was also inspected at the original short height before its regression was added.

The sixth full gate passed **1,529 unit, 113 integration and all 254 browser tests**, plus build, lint and documentation checks; Playwright took 33.2 minutes with one worker. The first repair landed as `e99d592d`, its outcome record as `b67c3433`. The served preview reached **697 commits**. The delivered reply reported the firefly behavior and completed repairs, while explicitly leaving the no-entry default unanswered. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-5); [delivered reply](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-6--november-2-the-3d-exploration-pass).

## Turn 6

The owner answered the remaining fork: begin in Structure when no entry is declared. They then reopened the 3D experience itself. Small discovered scenes remained difficult to navigate; orbit felt like the universe warped around the viewer; orphans and disconnected “microgalaxies” seemed missing. A direct 3D click should pin/unpin quickly before returning to detailed 2D.

They also described a rare ordinary 2D wire leaving a node to the right but travelling left toward its destination. Detecting that was a good reason to call attention to the 3D button. The test of the switch was explicit: if it failed to make the scene easier to understand, “not succeeded.” Forces and equations were in scope again, with common-sense polish expected.

The root accepted that direction while preserving explicit and remembered layouts. It separated two causes: a hop walk from one component cannot reach another, while per-object depth compensation changes actual world-space sizes during orbit. This permission did not select a previously parked shelf, hand-placed galaxy centers or hidden-node simulation. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-6--november-2-the-3d-exploration-pass).

## Turn 7

The root asked whether directory clicks should retain a local neighborhood or toggle whole-directory Open/Close: mapping every click to Open could reveal a repository unexpectedly. The owner accepted a simpler first experiment: a subsequent click should be able to release that node'"'s own contribution, even if another retained question still needs it. They expected a set of pins to be a common thing to share.

Files therefore use ordinary shared pins. Their release withdraws matching pin/discovery/subject contributions, while another question may keep the file visible. Directory clicks toggle temporary discovery; explicit Open/Close retains whole contents. No new directory-pin schema was invented. More → Inspect remains available, and the explicit Pin button retains an inspected but unpinned file rather than withdrawing its discovery.

The early pass also found and corrected a default-policy overreach: legacy links naming a file or directory must keep their Connections semantics. A fresh unrequested visit can use the entry-aware default; explicit/compressed and remembered choices still win. Leaving within the 150ms persistence debounce lost a deliberate choice, so page exit now flushes pending writes.

A later question offered Show all files versus a selective component browser. It had no explicit option answer at this stage; Turn 8 supplied broader discretion instead. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-7--reversible-contributions-to-the-explored-network). The supplementary export ends at this turn's user message.

## Turn 8

The owner asked the root to keep working until it felt comfortable presenting the 3D Local Map and its 2D transitions to new users trying to understand a directory, stopping only at an “abjectly an obvious user fork.” The root took the reversible overview first under that grant; it did not record an owner vote for that option or a numeric force setting.

The completed exploration pass includes:

- **A reversible whole overview.** Show all files adds the existing root opening, revealing disconnected groups and isolated files; Return to local scope removes only that opening. Independent directories, pins and discovery survive. Real members of components, including isolates, receive name priority. No canonical node or wire was hidden to improve the picture.
- **Rigid geometry through orbit.** Nodes, trimmed wire endpoints, arrows, shader width, picking, labels and framing use one zoom-compensation reference at the actual orbit target. The baseline orbit changed some uploaded world radii by **19.74%**; the shared-reference orbit changed none, with positions unchanged in both runs. GPU pixel checks retain perspective depth and cover clipping/tapers at two display scales. Their old antialiasing measurement was corrected without widening tolerances.
- **Emergent component spacing.** Group Separation weakens only component-centroid gravity toward the common origin while preserving internal gravity; exact-repulsion energy/force correspondence remains tested. No centers are placed by hand. Lowering global gravity alone was rejected by eye. Trials at 0.5/0.8 separated more but spent too much space or failed the existing re-easing contract. The final **0.2 is the agent's chosen balance**, not an owner-selected number. Stricter anchoring and opaque-canvas experiments were removed; no improvement is claimed for them. Kept corrections require completed reveal before cold anchoring and physical sphere extents for a lone file's settling scale.
- **Clicks that survive real hand motion.** Renderer and view share six pixels of click slop; a pressed label captures the pressed identity, so movement between press and release does not activate something else. Direct clicks add/withdraw contributions; other retained questions remain.
- **Directory discovery and correspondence.** Bounded containment neighborhoods carry into Connections without implicitly opening a subtree; collapsed directory aliases are resolved and deeper-only neighborhoods offer Open explicitly. Fit and layout changes use available room beside the reader. Directory-only and pin-only 2D/3D transitions run without inventing a selected file, revealing in-scope counterparts first. Unmatched folders fade as whole cards rather than floating names.
- **A visible scope control and a useful landing.** The estate's nine-state journey passed, but T3 still exposed dense scenes. Fresh Structure showed 118 files and recognizable landmarks. Following apps → server → src → mcp found the service through directory contents, yet MCP Connections expanded to 1,242 files. The existing Hops shown setting moved onto the toolbar, keeping the default two and its range: one hop brought that discovery to 116 files and the discovered service to 33. A pin-only return initially landed above the useful 2D cards; it now holds the newest pin's visible position without changing the selected subject.
- **A specific 2D cue.** Backward ordinary routes, including curve overshoot, outline the 3D control after layout settles. Self loops and intentional feedback stubs are excluded. A controlled displacement of actual cards exercised the real redraw and cue; it is not claimed as reproduction of the owner's still-unsupplied natural case.

The layout lab's scope was corrected to begin hops at the pins themselves and reveal whole-root files as directory members. Review caught an editing mistake: two added cases had overwritten three existing spatial-report cases. All three were restored unchanged before validation, and the two additions retained. A hand-drag test was also given a stimulus that actually stretches its spring, keeping its original movement and distance assertions.

The record distinguishes numerical and environmental evidence. In the software-rendered browser, whole-repository native readback/compositing dominated the profiling comparison; hiding labels did not remedy it, and neither hiding layers nor the opaque-context trial was adopted. T3's server discovery did not settle within a recorded 60-second wait, although further input worked. The final position is useful directory and local-pin exploration, not a claim of instant, legible whole-universe rendering.

The second round's fifth complete browser run finished **260/265**. Four failures were old test assumptions: counting Structure hubs as files, clicking a name to inspect, and two tuning inventories missing Group Separation. The real defect was the expanded pin tray's transparent layout rectangle intercepting History. Its actual surfaces remain interactive while the empty wrapper space no longer receives pointer input. The five corrected cases passed together after rebuilding and a private hit-target check.

The sixth complete gate then passed **1,541 unit, 113 integration and all 265 browser tests**, plus builds, lint and documentation audits; Playwright took **35.4 minutes**, one worker. Code/docs/evidence landed as "'`c91c019d`, the outcome record as `360d6af2`. Main and T3 clients were refreshed, and HTTP checks confirmed the final controls/CSS and all **699 main-history commits through `360d6af2`**. The owner'"'s shared tab was not driven. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-8--continue-until-the-directory-exploration-journey-is-ready-for-new-users); [playtests and recordings](../../../../Screenshots/2026-11-02/README.md); [measurements and rejected trials](../../../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/README.md).

## Turn 9

The owner thanked the agent and explicitly asked to close the dev-day chat, summarize it and preserve the preceding response/work before starting a new November 2 chat. The root recorded the delivered Turn 8 reply and this request verbatim, read both available captures in full, verified the commits, wrote this linked summary and updated the month inventory, narrative and current handoff/status. The source export's byte hash is preserved. This is an archival close-out; it does not reopen implementation or rerun the full application gate. The closing reply remains a capture gap until delivered and later recorded. [Source](../2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-9--close-the-november-12-development-session).

## Verified commits and outcomes

Verified with git on November 2. Attribution follows the conversation and changed-file scope, not dates alone.

| Commit | Subject | Role |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ["'`72601209`](https://github.com/jfjordanfarr/Live-Documentation/commit/726012099412e873326e60aaaf50f1459b7de7fe) | Review Explorer discovery and full-history usability | Turn 1, November 1: record, inventory and review evidence; no product source. |
| [`e99d592d`](https://github.com/jfjordanfarr/Live-Documentation/commit/e99d592d9af847b1ad6f1497a93b55efd7330839) | Repair Explorer history, discovery and directory framing | Turns 2–5, November 2: first implementation round and its verified evidence. |
| [`b67c3433`](https://github.com/jfjordanfarr/Live-Documentation/commit/b67c3433c08ccbc40536b3510742ac8c10ae4a82) | Record verified Explorer repairs and final preview | Turn 5 outcome; only the maintained record changed. |
| [`c91c019d`](https://github.com/jfjordanfarr/Live-Documentation/commit/c91c019d5b898c055641d1e8e49b753d88e37231) | Make 3D exploration reversible and preserve view orientation | Turns 6–8: second implementation round, docs, raw export and evidence. |
| [`360d6af2`](https://github.com/jfjordanfarr/Live-Documentation/commit/360d6af2554af9f59835280d51f3cd9a1c08b45b) | Record completed 3D exploration verification | Turn 8 outcome; only the maintained record changed. |

The close-out documentation commit follows these; no hash is invented here. No commit was amended or rewritten. The agent did not push. Source and all required checks were complete before this closing request; the working tree was clean at its arrival.

Evidence to reopen:

- [Original review](../../../../Screenshots/2026-11-01/README.md): 25 pictures and reproduction journeys.
- [First repair evidence](../../../../Screenshots/2026-11-01/remediation/README.md): 19 pictures, playback recording and timed RGB/alpha samples, with the 254-case gate.
- [Final exploration evidence](../../../../Screenshots/2026-11-02/README.md): 52 visual artifacts, with before/after orbit, estate, T3 and tray journeys clearly separated from rejected trials.
- [Exploration probe](../../../../Probes/2026-11-02/exploration/README.md): 29 reports/checks, including component trials, stability, actual buffer comparisons and frame costs.

## Open ends and handoff

- **Resolved and delivered:** the fresh no-entry 3D default is Structure; explicit/remembered and legacy subject choices retain their meaning. Direct 3D file clicks pin/release; directory discovery is reversible and Open remains whole-directory retention. The overview fork is no longer a blocker: the agent implemented the reversible existing-root entrance under Turn 8'"'s grant. The history ramp is removed. Do not import intermediate pending notes or rejected settings as current work.
- **Limits, not failed completion:** very large whole scopes remain dense; the T3 directory/one-hop journey is the useful entrance. Software-renderer measurements do not establish performance on every user's hardware. The owner has not yet supplied the natural backward-wire reproduction. Selective group browsers, hand-placed centers and hidden-node stabilization were not adopted.
- **Broader work remains where it belongs:** the installable package/newcomer rehearsal, T3's private-name and workspace-package-resolution gaps, and other longer-range questions remain in [direction](../../../../Memory/direction.md#next) and [the vision](../../../../../.mdmd/layer-1/vision.mdmd.md#the-order-of-work). They were not silently solved or newly selected by this UI pass. Follow the vision's order and current owner direction when asked what comes next.
- **Preview at the implementation handoff:** main at port 8900, 915 files, 2,252 file-pair edges, 92 related docs and 699 commits through "'`360d6af2`; T3 client over unchanged canonical data, 28,145 files/81,634 edges/51 related documents, no history. The owner controls their existing shared page. Subsequent archive-only commits may naturally lie beyond that recorded preview boundary.
- **Next chat:** use the ordinary narrative-plus-five-summaries bootstrap unless the owner overrides it. This session'"'s explicit all-October raw-read task is completed, not an instruction to repeat it by default. No implementation, full test run or private browser is left running from the completed pass.

### A review of the journey, firefly history, and a working set to share (November 1–2)

The owner opened November by asking Astra to read every October non-summary record and try the latest work as an end user: “tell me where it sucks for you as an end user.” The root read all twenty-seven records, rebuilt the complete 694-commit history and actually played it, then stopped on particular changes. Immediately before the January 12 removal of GraphStore, the picture still held the subsystem but search returned nothing for its name; the next step listed fifty-eight deleted files and 824 removed wires, with no action on a deleted file. Inspecting a neighbour grew the generator'"'s picture behind the reader, and closing the reader lost that growth. Opening Portal at an ordinary laptop size required Reset Zoom to recover its contents. T3's default was a knot of 9,341 files; a service name learned from the archive reduced it to 441 and a hidden tuning control reduced that to thirty-three. The [review](../Screenshots/2026-11-01/README.md) kept the successes too, including search ranking, reverse-path recovery and the reachable playback speed. It landed as a review alone, \\"'`72601209'"\\"'`. The owner'"'s answer made the next work plain: “we want to be able to look at directories and understand their contents well, especially if those contents have interconnectivity of some kind,” and “Go ahead and tackle the pain points as you see them.” ([Turns 1 and 2](../ChatHistory/2026/11/Summarized/2026-11-01.1.SUMMARIZED.md#turn-1)).

The repair reused the engine's existing lifetime and rename traversal so that historical search, pins, paths and shared addresses could name a file gone today without mistaking a later file at its old path for it. The reader said what the history could not supply: old source text and old symbol names. Discovery became state of its own, surviving reader closure. Directory unfolding kept its early anchor and then brought the useful contents above the floating controls; independent root columns aligned; uniform folder junctions replaced the large recursive ancestors. The timeline gained commit search, adjacent steps and a range that gave an interval room without dropping its surrounding commits. A requested camera frame followed growth until the user took over. Fresh-page Back exposed another failure, validation before history had loaded; another pass found a lost plain directory address. The repairs came from following those journeys through their boundaries, recorded with the failed gates and their causes ([Turn 2](../ChatHistory/2026/11/Summarized/2026-11-01.1.SUMMARIZED.md#turn-2)).

While the agent checked the build, the owner was playing it. Nodes grew brighter through an interval and suddenly dimmed at the next commit. The agent first described this as something that could “appear” to happen; the owner insisted it was unambiguous and that a human eye could see the temporal behaviour the still captures missed. The code settled the textual question: Dim actually raised unaffected opacity from a fifth to full before resetting it. The agent admitted the weak description. The owner then gave the picture to build: “a network of occasionally firing fireflies. Try to maximize surprise.” The clock was removed from emphasis. Changed nodes became white for the displayed commit against a steady background; the next event changed the participating set. Optional dimming stayed steady too. Actual uploaded colours and alpha were sampled at several points in intervals at one and half a step per second, through the next event, final commit and history close ([Turns 3–5](../ChatHistory/2026/11/Summarized/2026-11-01.1.SUMMARIZED.md#turn-3)). The first repair landed after midnight as \\"'`e99d592d'"\\"'`, with 1,529 unit, 113 integration and all 254 browser checks passing. A proposed exception to a startup assertion had been withdrawn when the agent found the real readiness-status lag; the original stricter assertion passed on the corrected code.

The owner accepted the pending choice to begin a fresh 3D visit in Structure when no entry was declared, then reopened 3D itself. Orbit felt as though the universe warped around the viewer; orphans and “microgalaxies” were missing; even small discovered scenes were difficult to navigate. Clicking a sphere should quickly add or remove a pin before returning to detailed 2D. A rare backward ordinary wire in 2D should call attention to the 3D button: if switching did not make the scene easier to understand, the software had “not succeeded.” When the root asked whether a directory click should retain a whole subtree, the owner chose a smaller contract: a subsequent click should withdraw that node'"'s own contribution, even if another retained question still needed it. A pin set might be a common thing to share ([Turns 6 and 7](../ChatHistory/2026/11/Summarized/2026-11-01.1.SUMMARIZED.md#turn-6)). This superseded the October inspect-on-click choice for 3D files. Inspection remained an explicit action; directory discovery could be released without silently opening everything below it.

The next cause was geometric. Compensation against each object's own depth changed its world-space size during orbit. Using the actual orbit target as one shared reference preserved geometry while retaining resistance to zoom: the recorded baseline changed some sphere radii by 19.74 percent, and the new drawing changed none. Disconnected files had never left the canonical graph, but no hop walk within one component could find another. The root asked about an overview or a group browser; the owner then asked it to continue until the directory journey was comfortable to present, stopping only at an “abjectly an obvious user fork.” Under that discretion the root made Show all files add the existing root opening and Return to local scope withdraw only it, preserving the person's other pins and directories. Component-centroid gravity became a public lever, preserving internal gravity and placing no centres by hand. The agent chose 0.2 after stronger trials spent too much space or moved too far on re-easing; it was an implementation judgment, never an owner-selected number. Lower global gravity, stricter anchoring and an opaque-canvas experiment were recorded and rejected ([Turn 8](../ChatHistory/2026/11/Summarized/2026-11-01.1.SUMMARIZED.md#turn-8)).

The complete journey exposed what the smaller repairs had not. Pin-only and directory-only scopes had bypassed the correspondence animation because there was no selected file. They now crossed with real identities, including closed folders becoming Structure hubs; unmatched folders faded as whole boxes. Real pointer jitter no longer cancelled a click, and a name that moved during a press kept the pressed identity. T3 began with 118 files and directory landmarks, and the root found a service through apps, server, source and MCP instead of arriving with its name already known. But the MCP Connections picture still became a 1,242-file knot. The existing Hops setting moved onto the toolbar, retaining the owner's default two; one hop showed 116 files for that discovery and thirty-three for the service. Returning that retained service to 2D first landed in empty space above its cards. Holding the newest pin visually, without making it the selected subject, corrected the landing. The [estate and T3 captures](../Screenshots/2026-11-02/README.md) retain those failures beside the eventual journeys, and the controlled backward-wire cue is labelled as a controlled reproduction, not the owner's unsupplied natural case.

The final gate found an invisible pin-tray rectangle intercepting History, which the drawing alone could not explain; only its actual panel and toggle now take pointer input. Four other browser failures were outdated premises, corrected while keeping their behavioral contracts. Review also caught two new layout-lab checks overwriting three old ones; the old tests were restored unchanged. The final complete run passed 1,541 unit, 113 integration and all 265 browser cases, one worker, and the work landed as \\"'`c91c019d'"\\"'`. The served history reached all 699 commits through its outcome record. The root judged ordinary directory and pin exploration ready to present, while preserving the dense large-scope and software-renderer limits; a successful local journey did not establish fluent whole-universe rendering. The owner closed the development day with thanks and asked for its [summary](../ChatHistory/2026/11/Summarized/2026-11-01.1.SUMMARIZED.md), now that November 2 had begun, so the next chat could carry forward both the work and what it had taught ([Turn 9](../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-01.1.record.md#turn-9--close-the-november-12-development-session)).

### Capture reading and archival decisions

- Read this maintained record in full through character 170,951 as it stood after the Turn 9 opening, and the supplementary export in full through character 312,849. Reading was in contiguous bounded slices, with checkpoints in the draft summary. The export's 313,392 bytes and SHA-256 "'`32c7b43c4f9fd5910f7f206414359517ccb68250b42440a051dfc5a123ec1509` are unchanged. It begins at Turn 1 and ends at the Turn 7 user message, followed by an empty `Codex:` prefix. It does not contain the later reply/work. Exported link labels sometimes lose their destinations and some progress text is grouped after tools; no exact timing was inferred from that placement.
- The final summary preserves all nine turns, both full verified implementation rounds, the owner'"'s temporal correction, rejected force/anchoring/context experiments, and the limits of large-scope/software-renderer observations. Git verified all five pre-close-out commits and their changed-file scope. The initial review remained separate from the later authorization to implement.
- Updated the narrative's last chapter, date and harness table; corrected the vision's still-pending no-entry statement from Turn 6's actual answer and Turn 8's completed implementation. The project's order of work remains unchanged. The sizing attribution is explicitly an interpretation of the November 1 wording in the context of October 28, rather than a fabricated explicit uniform-size instruction on November 1.
- Added summary-turn provenance to the authored portions of eleven affected Live Docs, including the two modules created for historical identity/navigation, temporal emphasis and the consequential 3D decisions. No generated region was edited by hand. Regeneration will reconcile derived citation/index data; no application source or test changes are made for this close-out.
- Two guarded archival editing attempts failed before writing: a JavaScript template literal with escaped backticks, and an exact model-table-row replacement that did not match. The narrative was then patched directly and a separate dated row added. These were editing-command errors, not product failures; no source content was lost.
